# Ars Belli Mod - Project Memory

This document provides context for AI agents working on the Ars Belli Mod for Europa Universalis V (EU5).
AI agents will keep this file updated from now on, and use caveman-speak to save up tokens. Care will be taken not to read big files directly unless necessary.

## Project Overview
Ars Belli Mod is a gameplay-focused mod primarily designed for multiplayer sessions. It introduces custom power ranking systems, diplomatic constraints, and extensive military rebalancing.

**Game Path:** `E:\Steam\steamapps\common\Europa Universalis V` (as of May 2026)

## Core Features & Systems

### 1. Multiplayer Power Ranking System (`mp_limits`)
The mod implements a custom ranking system that classifies countries into tiers based on their "Power Score".
- **Tiers:** Great Power (GP), Major Power, Normal, Small, Minor.
- **Scoring:** Calculated via `mp_limits_monthly_calculation` in `in_game\common\scripted_effects\ars_belli_scripted_effects.txt`. It considers:
    - Population score
    - Economic score
    - Manpower & Military strength
    - Navy (Sailors, Heavy Ships, Galleys)
    - Control & Vassal population
- **Classification:** Controlled by game rules (e.g., number of GPs/Majors) set at game start.
- **Triggers:** Use `is_mp_gp`, `is_mp_major`, etc., from `in_game\common\scripted_triggers\mp_limits_triggers.txt` to check a country's rank. `is_mp_top3_gp` = GP with `mp_rank_count < 3` (`mp_rank_count` = number of ranked countries with higher power score; unset/1000 for unranked).
- **Hegemon CB retargeted:** `cb_hegemon` ("Take Down Great Power") is a same-name replacement (`in_game\common\casus_belli\hegemon.txt`): target must be `is_mp_top3_gp`, actor must NOT be (mirrors vanilla's actor `is_hegemon = no`). Loc override in `replace\01_abm_locals_vanilla_l_english.yml`. War goal `take_capital_imperial` (0.70, shared with imperialism/revolution CBs). `cb_hegemon_ultimatum` kept vanilla.

### 2. Diplomatic Limits (Alliance & Defensive Points)
- Uses "Alliance Points" (AP) and "Defensive Points" (DP) to limit diplomatic web complexity in multiplayer.
- Rules are defined in `main_menu\common\game_rules\ars_belli_rules.txt` and localized in `main_menu\localization\english\ars_belli_rules_l_english.yml`.
- Penalties are applied via static modifiers if limits are exceeded.
- **Deduplication:** A country in both an alliance and a defensive league (or PU) with the player counts only once — under DP, not AP. Personal Unions count as defensive alliances (DP) but don't double-count if already in a defensive league. Logic in `in_game\common\script_values\mp_limits_values.txt`.
- **Display:** Player's own AP/DP/GP shown in `right_panel.gui` top bar. Foreign country AP/DP/GP shown in `foreign_country_lateralview.gui`. DP display shows actual (uncapped) values so overflow is visible.
- **Tier Country List Panel:** Click the rank label in `right_panel.gui` → opens an overlay panel listing all countries per tier (GP, Major, Normal, Small, Minor) with flag, name, score. No 20-entry cap.
    - **Storage**: `mp_limits_store_tier_lists` populates `mp_gp_list` / `mp_major_list` / `mp_normal_list` / `mp_small_list` / `mp_minor_list` global variable lists. Two-pass population (every_country into temp buffer `mp_tier_sort_buffer`, then `ordered_in_list` by `var:mp_power_score` to split into tier lists in score-desc order).
    - **Display**: Panel widget embedded in `right_panel.gui` (top|right anchor, search `# Ars Belli tier list panel`). Each tier section uses `dynamicgridbox` with `datamodel = "[GetGlobalList('mp_<tier>_list')]"` — vanilla pattern from `war_of_religions.gui`. Rows are clickable buttons (`OpenDiplomacy(Country.Self)`) with country tooltip.
    - **Toggle**: Per-player UI state via `GetVariableSystem` (client-side, not script vars). Rank label and close button both call `[GetVariableSystem.Toggle('mp_tier_panel_open')]`; panel `visible = "[GetVariableSystem.Exists('mp_tier_panel_open')]"`. Must NOT use global script vars here — those are shared across all MP players (one player opening the panel would open it for everyone). Pattern mirrors vanilla `court_and_country.gui` / `game_rules.gui`.
    - **Tooltip**: `MP_RANK_TOOLTIP` retains score breakdown + per-tier limits/counts (no longer lists countries). Counts via `[GetDataModelSize(GetGlobalList('mp_<tier>_list'))]`.

### 2b. Break Others' Guarantee
- Country interaction allowing a player to break another country's guarantee on them (hostile action).
- File: `in_game\common\country_interactions\break_others_guarantee.txt`
- Requires not being at war. Creates a truce on use.

### 2c. Worsen Opinion
- Country interaction allowing a player to worsen their own opinion of another country (-200 opinion, decays over 10 years). -200 is twice vanilla's Improve Relations cap (`opinion_improve_relation` = +100 in vanilla `biases\00_opinion_hardcoded.txt`) and is the floor of the opinion scale (`OPINION_MAX = 200`).
- Costs one diplomat and has a **1-month per-actor cooldown** (`cooldown = { type = worsen_opinion_cd months = 1 }`). Both exist because the action pushes a notification at the target on every use, and unthrottled spam at another player was reported to be able to crash their client. Per-actor, not per-target: a per-target cooldown still lets a player cycle every country in one sitting.
- Diplomat cost pattern (vanilla `country_interactions\form_closer_bond_iroquois.txt`): `allow = { scope:actor = { num_of_diplomats >= N } }` + `add_diplomats = -N` in the effect. Diplomats are a pool that regenerates at `monthly_diplomats` up to `max_diplomats` — not a permanent capacity, so spending them is a real but temporary cost.
- Cooldown loc key convention is `<cooldown_type>_cooldown` (e.g. `worsen_opinion_cd_cooldown`).
- Files: `in_game\common\country_interactions\worsen_opinion.txt`, `in_game\common\biases\ars_belli_opinion.txt`, loc in `main_menu\localization\english\00_mp_limits_l_english.yml`

### 2d. Sabotage Fort
- Covert action (`CATEGORY_COVERT_ACTIONS`, modelled on vanilla `sabotage_reputation`) costing **50 spy network** plus **gold scaled to the victim's income**.
- **Gold cost:** `price = price:abm_sabotage_fort_price` in `in_game\common\prices\abm_prices.txt`, using `scaled_recipient_gold = 3` = 3 months of `scope:recipient`'s monthly income (`scaled_recipient_gold`/`scaled_gold` = N months of recipient/actor income; vanilla documents this on `send_gift` in `prices\00_hardcoded.txt`). `min_scale = 25` floor, no `max_scale` on purpose. `payee` left unset so the gold leaves the game instead of paying the victim.
- Two select stages: country, then one of that country's locations with `has_fort = yes` and `is_capital = no` (`source = recipient` + `source_flags = only_actual_locations`, same as vanilla `seize_location_from_subject`). **Capitals are deliberately not targetable**; the country stage's `enabled` check mirrors the same filter so the tooltip does not offer countries whose only fort is their capital.
- Effect: `every_pop { limit = pop_type:soldiers; change_pop_type = pop_type:peasants }` in the target location. Forts declare `pop_type = soldiers`, so this leaves the fort unstaffed until the location promotes new soldiers on its own — temporary by design.
- `ai_tick = never` (every vanilla country_interaction with a location select stage is player-only).
- **Wartime only**: country stage `visible` has `is_at_war_with = scope:actor`. **2-year cooldown** per actor (`cooldown = { type = abm_sabotage_fort_cd years = 2 }`, loc `abm_sabotage_fort_cd_cooldown`) AND per victim: effect sets timed var `abm_sabotage_fort_victim_cd` (years = 2) on `scope:recipient`, country stage `enabled` greys the victim out (`abm_sabotage_fort_victim_cd_tt`). Engine `cooldown` is per actor only, hence the var.
- **Unlocked only by the Espionage focus pick** (Age of Discovery, the only espionage pick; swapped with Diplomatic, which is now Age of Absolutism): own advance `abm_sabotage_fort_advance` ("Sabotage Fort", 2nd of the branch, replaced `efficient_spies` whose +50% spy network construction moved onto root `vetting`) does only `unlock_country_interaction = abm_sabotage_fort` (set in `focus_data.js`, regenerate), plus `allow = { scope:actor = { has_advance = abm_sabotage_fort_advance } }` — same pattern as `steal_technology` / `abm_industrial_espionage`.
- **Victim is notified.** `show_message` / `show_message_to_target` left at default, so the engine raises `ACTION_abm_sabotage_fort_PERFORMED_ON_US_*` on the fort owner naming the actor and the location. Those message keys (plus the `WE_PERFORM_*` set) must be present in the loc file or the popup renders raw key names. Both types are also registered in the ABM block of `main_menu\gui\messagetypes.txt` (`abm_sabotage_fort` in `.tools\scripts\sync_messagetypes.py` ABM_TYPES), which gives them a toggle in message settings.
- Files: `in_game\common\country_interactions\abm_sabotage_fort.txt`, cost in `in_game\common\diplomatic_costs\abm_diplo_costs.txt`, loc `in_game\localization\english\abm_sabotage_fort_l_english.yml`. Message-type loc (`WE_PERFORM_*`, `ACTION_*_PERFORMED_ON_US_*`) lives in MAIN_MENU `abm_messages_l_english.yml` (messagetypes load before in_game loc -> "TODO : Write a desc" if in in_game). Price needs modifier type `abm_sabotage_fort_price_cost_modifier` (`main_menu\common\modifier_type_definitions\abm_sabotage_fort.txt`, loc in abm_modifiers_l_english.yml). none_available texts start `@trigger_no! `.

### 3. War & Military Rebalancing
- **Ticking Warscore:** Max 36 at +1/month (reduced from 50 because occupations give double warscore post-patch). Defined in `loading_screen\common\defines\01_ars_belli_defines.txt`.
- **Unconditional Surrender:** Removed (base game now has one). Previously in `in_game\common\country_interactions\unconditional_surrender.txt`.
- **Intervene in War / Threaten War / engine Enforce Peace:** all three removed, replaced by the scripted `ars_belli_enforce_peace`. See 3j.
- **Restore Roman Borders CB nerfed (2026-11-08):** `REPLACE:superiority_restore_roman_borders` in `in_game\common\wargoals\abm_wargoals.txt`, attacker `conquer_cost` 0.5 -> 0.6 (-50% -> -40%). Subjugate and defender left vanilla 0.5. Peace deal cost also stacks engine `PEACE_COST_EFFICIENCY_*` (same culture/religion, rival), so in-game discount reads bigger than the CB value.

### 3b. Fort Rebalancing
Significant changes to siege mechanics and fort limits (documented in `changes.txt` and `loading_screen\common\defines\01_ars_belli_defines.txt`):
- **Fort Limit:** Base limit of 5. Affected by tech and locations, NOT country rank or city count.
- **Defensiveness:** Removed movement speed/combat bonuses; focuses on scaling siege ability penalties.
- **Offensiveness:** Grants Logistics Distance, Assault Ability, and Siege Ability.

### 3e. Access Diplomacy
- **"Pay for X" sliders:** country_interactions in `in_game\common\country_interactions\abm_pay_for_access.txt` — now only `pay_for_trade_access` and `pay_for_fondaco_rights`. Each uses `select_trigger { looking_for_a = value ... }` (pattern from `bribe_voter_for_policy`) to expose a gold slider; default 12 months of recipient income, min 0, max actor's gold. `effect` transfers gold actor→recipient and creates the relation. `ai_will_do = -1000`. They need enough offered gold (8 acceptance points per month of recipient income) to convince the AI.
- **REMOVED 2026-10-24: `pay_for_military_access`, `pay_for_fleet_basing_rights`, `pay_for_food_access`** — arbitrary-amount slider + auto-accept (base 1000) made them a gold-transfer exploit between players. Loc, `abm_messages` keys and `main_menu\gui\messagetypes.txt` blocks removed too. Trade/fondaco sliders have the same transfer shape — user only asked for the 3; flagged.
- **Vanilla requests now cost no favors:** same-name replacement `in_game\common\diplomatic_costs\00_hardcoded.txt` empties `military_access` / `fleet_basing_rights` (vanilla `favors = 5`; empty block per vanilla `sell_icon_cost`). Not REPLACE: (unverified in this db). Food access never had a favor cost. `annullment_favours_required = 5` (annul via favors) untouched. Vanilla `buy_price` paths (`buy_military_access` price, engine-scaled, max 400) still exist.
- No GUI edits — country_interactions auto-appear in the diplomacy panel under their category.

### 3n. Trade Office Rights - player-to-player gate on `trade_office` (2026-10-15)
- **Copied from vanilla `fondaco`:** the `fondaco` building requires the location owner to be giving `fondaco_rights` in its `allow` block (`owner = { giving_scripted_relation = { type = relation_type:fondaco_rights target = scope:actor } }`; in `allow` root is the LOCATION, so `owner` = location owner and `scope:actor` = builder). Trade offices now work the same way through a new relation.
- **New relation `abm_trade_office_rights`** (`in_game\common\scripted_relations\abm_trade_office_rights.txt`): oneway, `break_on_war = yes`, `CATEGORY_ECONOMY_ACTIONS`, 1:1 with `fondaco_rights` - the loc key set is exactly the 50 vanilla fondaco keys, renamed. Revoking/breaking does NOT destroy existing offices (same as fondacos).
- **PLAYER-ONLY by design.** `offer_visible`/`request_visible` require BOTH `scope:actor` and `scope:recipient` to be `is_ai = no`, and `should_ai_offer_trigger = { always = no }` - a player can never offer/request rights with an AI and the AI never starts one. `visible = { always = yes }` is deliberate so an existing relation can still be cancelled/broken if a side turns AI.
- **Building gate is conditional:** `REPLACE:trade_office` (`in_game\common\building_types\abm_foreign_buildings.txt`, new file) only demands the relation inside `trigger_if = { limit = { scope:actor = { is_ai = no } owner = { is_ai = no } } }`. AI builder OR AI-owned location => requirement skipped, so everything involving an AI is byte-for-byte vanilla behaviour. `allow_wrong_startup = yes` is kept so the inherited vanilla setup placements still work (same reason as kurultai/order_commandery). The check sits in a `custom_tooltip` (`abm_trade_office_rights_required_tt`) so the greyed-out build button explains itself.
- **`is_ai`, not `is_player`** - EU5 has no `is_player` trigger; the player check is `is_ai = no` (vanilla precedent: `theodosian_walls` in `unique_buildings.txt`, `irrigation_systems` in `common_buildings.txt`).
- **Loc:** `in_game\localization\english\abm_trade_office_rights_l_english.yml`. Relation action keys are `offer_/request_/cancel_/break_<name>` - `cancellation_` is NOT a relation key (vanilla has 297 `cancel_` and 0 `cancellation_` in `scripted_relations_l_english.yml`).

- **The enforcing half - "Expel Trade Offices"** (`in_game\common\country_interactions\abm_expel_trade_offices.txt`, loc `abm_expel_trade_offices_l_english.yml`, 2026-10-16). The host destroys EVERY `trade_office` the target country holds in the host's own locations and revokes the `abm_trade_office_rights` relation in the same action (revoking alone leaves the buildings standing by design, so the target would just rebuild). Unilateral, so it is modelled on `worsen_opinion` - the mod's only other unilateral country action - and NOT on the offer-style `pay_for_*`: no `accept`, no `reject_effect`, no `reject_effect_text` loc. Costs one diplomat the way `worsen_opinion` does: `num_of_diplomats >= 1` in `allow` plus `add_diplomats = -1` inside the effect guard. Player-only on BOTH sides (`is_ai = no` on actor and recipient) so AI holdings stay vanilla. **Fondacos got the identical action** (`abm_expel_fondacos.txt` + its own loc file, same shape) - only the building type and the relation differ, and it targets the VANILLA `fondaco_rights`, which is the only way to clear a fondaco that is already standing since revoking the rights only stops new ones.
- **Recipe: destroy ANOTHER country's building in your land.** Vanilla's only precedent is `peace_treaties\disband_kontor.txt` (Hanseatic kontors), and it needs two pieces vanilla uses nowhere else: `location_building_level = { building_type = building_type:<x> value > 0 owner = scope:recipient }` to *find* one specific country's building in a location (the `owner` argument is what filters by owner), and `destroy_building_forcefully = "building(building_type:<x>|scope:recipient)"` to remove it - plain `destroy_building` is for your own buildings. Wrap in `every_owned_location` to sweep the whole realm. The `building(<type>|<owner-scope>)` selector is the crux; other vanilla uses are `generic_actions\catholic.txt` (seat_of_cardinal|owner), `japanese_shogunate.txt` (kokufu|scope:target) and `golden_age_of_piracy.txt` (|prev).

### 3o. Trade Office level cap: flat per-rank (2026-10-15)
- **Cap is mod-owned and deliberately flat.** `abm_trade_office_max_level` in `in_game\common\script_values\abm_building_caps.txt` = `add 1` then `if megalopolis add 2 / else_if city add 1` -> **town (and rural) 1, city 2, megalopolis 3** (those are the TOTALS base+bonus; retuned 2026-10-27, was 1/3/5). No development, no population, no owner-modifier, no division. `if/else_if` (not two separate `if`s) matters: a megalopolis must not also match the city branch. Vanilla's `trade_office_max_level` instead scaled off `development*0.1` and `population*0.05` and landed ~10 in a city.
- **Why a mod-owned value rather than a same-name override:** `REPLACE:` is per-database and silent when unsupported, so the building points at this value (`REPLACE:trade_office` -> `max_levels = abm_trade_office_max_level`). The vanilla `trade_office_max_level` is untouched and now unreferenced by anything.
- **Setup reduced to fit** (`main_menu\setup\1337\07_cities_and_buildings.txt`, already a same-name replacement): 22 offices sat above level 1 (16 mod Silk Road ABKHI/ABKAS at 3-5, 6 vanilla Mali GRA at 2-4). Now **cities -> 2, towns -> 1** (a `rank = town` location has no rank bonus, so it can only ever hold 1). Re-clamped 2026-10-27 for the 1/2/3 curve: the 15 level-3 city offices (ABKHI/ABKAS Silk Road, GRA Mali) dropped to 2. Final: 58x level 1, 16x level 2 (all cities), nothing higher - so nothing is above its cap. Above max level the engine only logs `building_manager.cpp:136: <building> in <loc> is above max level!` at startup and keeps the building; it does not delete or downscale it.
- **`fondaco` is a flat ONE, and unique per location (2026-10-16, tightened 2026-10-27).** `REPLACE:fondaco` (same file as `REPLACE:trade_office`) sets `max_levels = 1` directly - no script value, no rank bonus, never upgradeable. Vanilla read the **SHARED** `guild_max_level` (read by **30** guild/marketplace buildings in `common_buildings.txt`, `production_*.txt`, `trade_buildings.txt`), so it could not be retuned in place; `abm_fondaco_max_level` briefly existed in `script_values\abm_building_caps.txt` and was removed once the cap became a constant. `allow` also enforces one per location for EVERYONE: `NOT = { any_foreign_buildings_in_location = { building_type = building_type:fondaco NOT = { owner = scope:actor } } }` behind `custom_tooltip = abm_fondaco_unique_per_location_tt`. The `owner` filter matters - without it the holder's own fondaco would block its own upgrades - though with `max_levels = 1` there are no upgrades left to block. Note `allow` only gates BUILDING: script, setup data and `change_building_owner` bypass it, so a location can only gain a second fondaco deliberately. `country_has_no_other_copy_of_building` is per-COUNTRY and is not the tool for per-location limits. Only 4 fondacos exist in the 1337 setup (GEN, all level 1), so no setup clamp was needed.
- **hanseatic_kontor joins the same slot (2026-10-27).** HSA is excluded from fondacos, so its kontor is the Hansa's equivalent. `REPLACE:hanseatic_kontor` (third block in `abm_foreign_buildings.txt`) is vanilla verbatim plus `max_levels = 1` (vanilla was 3 base / +2 city / +10 megalopolis, then x `market_building_levels`, i.e. 5-13 levels - the value `hanseatic_kontor_max_level` is now unreferenced) and a NEW `allow` (vanilla had none) enforcing the shared slot: `building_type = fondaco OR hanseatic_kontor`, `NOT = { owner = scope:actor }`. `REPLACE:fondaco`'s check was widened the same way, and the tooltip key is now `abm_fondaco_slot_taken_tt` ("This location already has a Fondaco or Hanseatic Kontor"). So a location holds ONE trade post of either kind, whoever owns it. Setup: all 37 HSA kontors clamped to level 1 (22 were 2-3).
- **All four trade posts are `expensive = yes` (2026-10-27).** Building gold cost is NOT in the building definition: the engine charges the age price id `p_building_age_*` from `in_game\common\prices\01_buildings.txt`, resolved through `build_price_age_of_tradition..revolution` in `main_menu\common\script_values\default_values.txt` (= 50/100/200/400/800/1200). `expensive = yes` switches the building to the `p_expensive_building_age_*` table (200/400/800/1600/3200/5000) - one line, keeps age scaling, and is what 181 vanilla buildings use. Alternatives if a finer knob is ever needed: `price = <bare id>` (NOT `price:<id>`; vanilla examples `hre_army_building`, `expensive_estate_building`) backed by a mod `prices/` entry, and `construction_demand` (goods bill - note `trade_office` has NONE, so it costs zero goods to build while fondaco/enclave use `fondaco_construction` and kontor uses `kontor_construction`). `increase_per_level_cost` is inert on the three max_levels=1 buildings and only affects trade_office upgrades. Never edit `p_building_age_*` itself - that reprices every building in the game.
- **Merchant Enclave (`abm_merchant_enclave`) - the fourth trade post, 2026-10-27.** New plain building (not a REPLACE) in `abm_foreign_buildings.txt`, structurally identical to fondaco - same employment `foreign_trade_employment`, `fondaco_maintenance` PM, `fondaco_construction`, `trade_post_build_time`, `max_levels = 1`, same naval-range + `fondaco_rights` gate - with modifiers at ~75%: merchant_power 1.5, capacity 0.4, maritime 0.04, migration 0.04, raw `local_monthly_development` 0.001. `country_potential` = `has_reform` for five ABM reforms: `abm_china_meritocratic_government`, `abm_india_maritime_league`, `abm_india_bazaar_republic`, `abm_bengaleese_meritocracy`, `abm_trade_emporium`. The one-per-location check now lists all three types (`fondaco`, `hanseatic_kontor`, `abm_merchant_enclave`) in all four blocks, and "Expel Fondacos, Kontors and Enclaves" destroys all three.
- **Vanilla `fondaco_rights` had to be shadowed for this.** Its `visible` block only allows the RECIPIENT to be an Italian-culture `is_merchant_republic` or have `ara_consulate_sea`, so none of the five ABM reforms could ever be granted the rights - the enclave would have been unbuildable. The mod now ships a same-name copy at `in_game\common\scripted_relations\fondaco_rights.txt` (vanilla verbatim apart from five added `has_reform` lines in that OR; diffs clean) and it is listed in `replaced_files.txt`. Learn the general lesson: **before gating a new object on an existing scripted relation, read that relation's `visible`/`offer_visible` - they often restrict who can receive it.** The relation's vanilla loc was overridden in `abm_trade_office_rights_l_english.yml` to mention the Merchant Enclave.
- **Expelling has consequences (2026-10-27).** `abm_expel_fondacos`'s effect now also: gives the VICTIM vanilla's `cb_destroyed_building` against the actor (`add_casus_belli = { target = scope:actor type = casus_belli:cb_destroyed_building years = 10 }`) and puts the country modifier `abm_expelled_foreign_merchants` on the ACTOR for 10 years (`trade_income` -10%, `global_merchant_capacity_modifier` -10%, `trade_range_modifier` -10%; `mode = add_and_extend` so repeats extend). The CB lives in vanilla `casus_belli\00_hardcoded.txt` ("generate through code", `create_visible = { always = no }`, `war_goal_type = superiority`) - **hidden CBs are script-grantable**: 33 of vanilla's 44 `create_visible = no` CBs are added by `add_casus_belli` somewhere (e.g. `cb_insulted_us`, `cb_destabilized_our_state`), even though `cb_destroyed_building` itself is never script-added in vanilla. Modifier defined in `main_menu\common\static_modifiers\abm_country_changes.txt`, loc (`STATIC_MODIFIER_NAME_/DESC_`) in `main_menu\localization\english\abm_modifiers_l_english.yml`.
- **"Expel Fondacos and Kontors"** - `abm_expel_fondacos` now also destroys `hanseatic_kontor` (same `location_building_level` + `destroy_building_forcefully` pattern, second `if` block), since a kontor occupies the slot a host may want back. Name/desc/effect loc updated; the internal action name is unchanged.
- **fondaco retuned 2026-10-27:** `country_potential` is now plain `is_merchant_republic = yes` + `NOT = { has_or_had_tag = HSA }` (vanilla's Italian-culture-group requirement and the `ara_consulate_sea` policy path are gone; the old condition is left commented out), all four `modifier` values are doubled (merchant_power 2.0, maritime presence 0.05, merchant_capacity 0.5, local_migration_attraction 0.05), `increase_per_level_cost` 0.5 -> 1.0, and it keeps `raw_modifier = { local_monthly_development = 0.001 }`. Find a "shared value I want to change for one building only" by counting `max_levels = <value>` hits first: `guild_max_level` = 30 consumers, `trade_office_max_level` = 1.
- **Offline facts worth keeping** (needed for any future `*_max_level` work): location ranks are declared as `\t<location> = { rank = town|city|megalopolis town_setup = ... }` near the top of `07_cities_and_buildings.txt`; `population` is scaled **1 unit = 1000 actual pops** (`CONQUER_DESIRE_POPULATION_FACTOR`, `loading_screen\common\defines\00_defines.txt`); `development` is the **SUM of every matching key** in `main_menu\setup\1337\14_development.txt` (base -2, city +5 / town +2, climate, topography, vegetation, region ~10-30, area, province, per-location overrides).

- **Trade office cap is PER OWNER, not per location (2026-10-31).** Town 1, city 2, megalopolis 3 applies to each country separately, so ten countries may each hold a 2-level office in the same city (20 levels in one location is legal). A building entity is keyed on `(building_type, owner)` within a location - that is what `destroy_building = "building(building_type:x|owner)"` relies on - so an office's OWN level is its owner's level there, and per-owner checking needs nothing more than `building_level`.
- **Over-cap safety net.** `max_levels` is only enforced when something is BUILT or UPGRADED, so an office can still end up over its owner's cap (a city falling to a town with a level-2 office left standing; `change_building_level_in_location` from other script; a `change_building_owner` merge). `scripted_triggers/abm_building_triggers.txt` `abm_trade_office_above_cap` (root = building) answers "is my own level above the cap my location's rank allows", and `scripted_effects/abm_building_effects.txt` trims it: `abm_trim_trade_office_self` (root = building, two passes) and `abm_trim_trade_offices_in_location` (root = location, every building judged on its own owner). Hooks in `on_action/abm_building_on_actions.txt`: `on_building_built` (root = building) and `on_location_changed_rank` (root = location). Two passes suffice - max level 3, min cap 1.
- **A first attempt used `location_building_level` and was WRONG** - that is the location TOTAL, so it would have trimmed a city holding ten players' legal offices down to 2 levels total. Never use location totals for this cap.
- **`hsa_burgtor` inspected as a precedent (2026-10-31).** `unique_buildings.txt:6280` - a foreign building, `max_levels = 2`, `city`/`megalopolis` only, with the richest `remove_if` in vanilla: culture group of `location.owner` no longer matches HSA, `location = { has_owner = no }`, or `location.owner ?= { is_at_war_with = root.owner }`. Three things it establishes: (1) `remove_if` root IS the building, so `root.owner` and `location.owner` are read directly; (2) `remove_if` is a LIVE self-check, not a one-off at build time - otherwise the war clause would be meaningless; (3) it has NO rank clause, so vanilla does not auto-remove a building when its location drops below the ranks it allows - our `rural_settlement` remove_if is the analogue burgtor lacks. It also does no level trimming at all, so it is not a substitute for the effect + on_action.
- **`building_level` is per-ENTITY and valid in building scope.** Vanilla proof: `any_buildings_in_location = { building_type = building_type:granary  building_level >= 5 }` (town_rights/byzantine_greek_roman.txt) and `"building(building_type:great_temple_of_tenochtitlan|owner)" = { building_level <= 6 }` (flavor_AZT.txt). This is what makes the per-owner cap check work without any location-total arithmetic. Note `change_building_level` (building-scope effect, building_effects.txt) exists but is only used in one debug event with a bare integer, so whether it SETS or ADDS is unproven - keep using `change_building_level_in_location` with `value = -1`, which vanilla uses as a proven delta.- **`remove_if` cannot trim.** It deletes the whole building entity, so a 2/1 office would fall to 0/1, not 1/1. It IS used for the genuine cap-0 case in `REPLACE:trade_office`: `location ?= { location_rank = location_rank:rural_settlement }` (`rural_settlement = no` on the building, but the engine keeps buildings in locations that drop below their allowed rank; vanilla precedent `unique_buildings.txt`).
- Both hooked on_actions are EMPTY in vanilla 1.4, so nothing vanilla is lost whether EU5 merges on_action blocks by name or replaces them. Precedent that same-name on_action blocks coexist: `on_action/abm_mp_limits_on_actions.txt` redefines `monthly_country_pulse`, which vanilla fills with its own list.
- Untested in game. No sweep runs at load, so an office already over its owner's cap in an existing save is only corrected when something in that location is built or re-ranked.
- **Rank demotion WIPES a location's buildings (2026-10-31, confirmed in play by the user).** Demoting a city or megalopolis destroys the location's buildings and leaves a rural settlement, so an office can never survive a cap-dropping rank change. That removed the only real reason the whole over-cap trim existed (see below), and it was deleted.
- **FINAL SHAPE: no trim code at all.** `REPLACE:trade_office` in `building_types/abm_foreign_buildings.txt` keeps `max_levels = abm_trade_office_max_level` (engine-enforced per building entity, so per owner, at build and upgrade time) plus a rural-settlement `remove_if` that is now belt-and-braces. Everything else built during this work was deleted: `scripted_effects/abm_building_effects.txt`, `scripted_triggers/abm_building_triggers.txt`, `on_action/abm_building_on_actions.txt`, `abm_trade_office_max_level_building` in `script_values/abm_building_caps.txt`, the audit decision and its localisation, both world sweeps, the one-shot global-flag guard, and the edit to `abm_mp_limits_on_actions.txt`. `abm_building_caps.txt` and `abm_mp_limits_on_actions.txt` are byte-identical to their committed versions.
- **Historical notes, so none of this is re-invented:** a world walk `every_country -> every_owned_location -> every_buildings_in_location` FROZE the game with no error-log entry (volume, not a fault); `every_owned_foreign_building` misses offices a country holds in its OWN locations because `is_foreign` only applies abroad; and `"building(type|owner)"` ERRORS rather than returning false when the building is absent, so it must be guarded with `limit = { has_building = building_type:<type> }`. All moot for this feature now, but they are the traps that cost the most time.
- **Console script execution:** `effect {effect script}`, `run {effect file}` (reads `run/<name>.txt`; errors "Not a .txt" and "No such file in /run"), `trigger {trigger script}`. `effect` writes `Executing effect script "..."` / `Effect is empty` to error.log, so a failed scope is visible. `remove_global_variable = <name>` takes a bare name, not a block.- **Modifier beneficiary depends on the modifier, not the block.** The trade office's `modifier` block is location-scope, so `merchant_power_from_building` / `merchant_capacity_from_building` / `building_owner_maritime_presence` read "to the **building** owner" (the foreign trader - confirmed by `MODIFIER_TYPE_DESC_*` in `modifier_types_l_english.yml`), while `local_market_access = 0.03` (raised from 0.01 on 2026-10-27) is the marketplace's own value and reads "the **[location]** would get" - so it benefits the **host** (the country that grants the rights), not the trader. `local_market_access` (`category=location`, `percent=yes`) is the ONLY market-access modifier in the game - `grep` over `modifier_type_definitions\00_modifier_types.txt` finds no country-scope equivalent, so "market access for the building owner" is not expressible. **Same block, two scaling rules:** `modifier` is multiplied by building level *and* goods access, `raw_modifier` is not scaled by either - the trade office's `local_monthly_prosperity = 0.001` (and the fondaco's `local_monthly_development = 0.001`) therefore sits in `raw_modifier` so one office pays a flat amount however many levels it has. (`local_monthly_development` is `category=location`, `decimals=4`, **not** `percent` - it is a flat development/month value; the `percent=yes` sibling is `local_monthly_development_modifier`.)

### 3d. Force Break Union
- Any union member (senior, junior, or federal peer) gets a **Force Break Union** button in the union panel.
- **Effect:** spends 20 legitimacy (`legitimacy_extreme_penalty`), removes the actor from the union IO (`remove_country_from_international_organization`), then generates a random new ruler via `create_character` matching the actor's culture/religion and calls `set_new_ruler`.
- **Limit:** 50-year per-actor cooldown (`cooldown = { type = force_break_union_cd years = 50 }`). Legitimacy gating surfaces via the `force_break_union_legitimacy_tt` custom_tooltip in `allow`. `ai_tick = never`.
- **Why `generic_action` (type=owncountry), not country_interaction:** the action only affects the actor — country_interactions force a recipient `select_trigger` step in the UI ("pick a country" panel) that is meaningless here. Generic_actions with `type = owncountry` need no recipient and no select_trigger.
- **GUI button** uses `left_click_and_hold_action = { action_name = "force_break_union" }` (click-and-hold acts as a built-in confirmation). No `action_direction`, no IO `parameter` — those are country_interaction concepts.
- **Files:** `in_game\common\generic_actions\ars_belli_force_break_union.txt`, GUI button hand-placed in `in_game\gui\panels\organization\union.gui` (after `place_relative_on_throne` in MAIN_ACTIONS — search for `# Ars Belli force_break_union button`), tooltip template `io_force_break_union_button_tooltip` in `in_game\gui\shared\ars_belli_force_break_union.gui`, loc strings in `in_game\localization\english\ars_belli_force_break_union_l_english.yml`.
- **GUI override:** `union.gui` is a full-file copy (added to `replaced_files.txt`); reapply the mod block after `place_relative_on_throne` when vanilla updates.

### 3c. Crusade & Jihad
- **Targeting:** Crusades can now hit any heathen kingdom/empire whose capital is in `continent:europe`, `sub_continent:north_africa`, or `sub_continent:middle_east`, OR holds a Catholic holy site (vanilla path). Override of `is_valid_target_for_crusade` in `in_game\common\scripted_triggers\crusade_target_overrides.txt`.
- **Global cooldown:** Both Crusade and Jihad limited to once per 100 years globally per religion. Enforced via global vars `crusade_recently_called_global` / `jihad_recently_called_global` (set in effect with `years = 100`), checked through scripted triggers `crusade_global_cooldown` / `jihad_global_cooldown` (same overrides file). Per-actor `cooldown` block also bumped to 100 for UI consistency.
- **Files:** `in_game\common\resolutions\abm_crusade.txt` (REPLACE:call_crusade), `in_game\common\generic_actions\muslim.txt` (same-name full-file override of vanilla, which holds only call_jihad; was REPLACE:call_jihad in abm_jihad.txt until 2026-10-28, but REPLACE: does not bind in generic_actions). Cooldown loc strings in `in_game\localization\english\ars_belli_crusade_l_english.yml`.

### 3f. Economic Support (scripted replacement)
- Vanilla "Send Economic Support" is **engine-only** — not a relation_type, no scripted_relation / country_interaction / on_action / ai_diplochance entry, and its action list comes from `Country.GetQuickDiplomaticActions`. It cannot be removed or hooked, so it is relabelled "AGAINST THE RULES" in loc (`ECONOMIC_SUPPORT*` keys) and a replacement was built alongside it.
- **Split across two files on purpose:** the relation `ars_belli_economic_support` (`in_game\common\scripted_relations\`) is the ongoing half; the amount slider + accept/decline popup live in the country_interaction `ars_belli_send_economic_support` (`in_game\common\country_interactions\`). A relation's own `select_trigger` is the only selection stage in its flow, so a value-type one leaves the picker with no attribute columns and the game asserts on `(AttributeColumns.GetSize() > 0)` and crashes. The interaction's country stage supplies columns first. Hence `offer_visible/request_visible = { always = no }` on the relation.
- **Amount storage:** the agreed figure is a variable `ab_ecosupport_amount` on the RECIPIENT (unambiguous because the interaction allows only one patron at a time). Set to 0 then `change_variable add = scope:target_value` — assigning the selection scope directly can store the object instead of the number. `gold_to_second = ars_belli_economic_support_amount` reads it back. Cleared by cancel/break/expire (each uses a different scope — actor / actor / `scope:second`).
- **Cap on the amount** (`in_game\common\script_values\ars_belli_economic_support_values.txt`, `# --- tune here ---` block at the top): the slider max is the lower of a share of the RECIPIENT's `country_tax_base` (currently 1.0) and a share of the SENDER's `monthly_income_total` (currently 10.0, a backstop), floored at 1 gold, opening at half the cap. Scaling both shares together moves the ceiling; their ratio decides how small a recipient must be before its own economy is the limit. **The shares are spelled out in words in the loc file** (`ars_belli_economic_support_relation_desc`, `ars_belli_send_economic_support_desc`) — retune those texts alongside.
- `country_tax_base` is the country-scope value for Tax Base and works as `scope:recipient.country_tax_base` inside a value `select_trigger`'s min/max/default (vanilla precedent: `bribe_vote.txt`). Guard it with `exists = scope:recipient` — `ai_override_value` can be evaluated with no recipient in scope. Pass `max` a plain number or a single value reference; vanilla never inlines arithmetic into `max = { ... }`.
- Costs both sides a Defensive Point (see `mp_limits` weight recompute) and -0.10 monthly diplomats via `giving_`/`receiving_` auto-modifiers in `main_menu\common\static_modifiers\ars_belli_country_changes.txt`. `ai_tick = never` on the interaction.

### 3f2. Tributaries excluded from transfers
- A tributary can no longer appear on either end of `give_subject_location_to_other_subject` or `transfer_subject`. Checks sit in the `enabled` block of each selection stage (`custom_tooltip` + `NOT = { is_subject_type = tributary }`), so the country greys out with a reason instead of vanishing from the picker.
- Both are same-name full-file replacements of vanilla country_interactions (in `replaced_files.txt`); shared loc key `ars_belli_not_a_tributary_tt` in `in_game\localization\english\ars_belli_tributaries_l_english.yml`.

### 3g. Union war participation (call to arms)
- IO war-joining is controlled by six fields on the international_organization, documented in vanilla `international_organizations\readme.txt` lines 94-99. For each of defensive/offensive: `join_*_wars_always` (silently auto-join, no prompt), `join_*_wars_auto_call` (**call to arms is issued automatically, the callee still accepts/declines**), `join_*_wars_can_call` (a call may be made manually). Scopes: root = the IO, `scope:actor` = caller, `scope:recipient` = callee, `scope:target` (optional) = against who.
- Vanilla `union.txt` had `join_defensive_wars_always = { always = yes }`, commented as the baseline of a union that "will never change" — union partners were dragged into each other's defensive wars with no popup. Ars Belli sets it to `no` and adds `join_defensive_wars_auto_call = { always = yes }`, which is what produces the accept/decline popup. Vanilla precedents for auto_call: `hre.txt`, `ilkhanate.txt`.
- **The offensive side is untouched and is not in `union.txt` at all** — it is voted through `laws\40_personal_unions.txt` → `union_mutual_offense_law`: Assured Offense sets `join_offensive_wars_always`, Automatic Offense sets `join_offensive_wars_auto_call`, Possible Offense sets `join_offensive_wars_can_call`. There is no defensive equivalent law, so the hardcoded baseline in `union.txt` is the only defensive lever.
- `union.txt` is a same-name full-file replacement (in `replaced_files.txt`).

### 3h. Sell / Buy Location — engine-locked except a flat GUI ceiling
- The sell/buy location action is **entirely engine-implemented**, like vanilla Economic Support was (see 3f). Confirmed absent: no `country_interactions` file, no price id in `prices\00_hardcoded.txt` (checked against the full several-hundred-entry list), no `diplomatic_costs` entry, and no on_action of its own. Only `ai_diplochance\00_ai_diplochance.txt` (`sell_location` / `buy_location` AI weights) and the GUI are moddable.
- **`on_location_changed_owner` is the only ownership hook** (root = location, `scope:loser` = previous owner, `scope:winner` = new owner) and it **cannot tell a sale from a conquest, a peace-treaty transfer, a gift to a subject, or any of the ~155 scripted `change_location_owner` calls in vanilla**. Do not use it to hang costs on "selling" — it will silently tax event-driven transfers. This is why the requested -10 prestige on sale/purchase was **not implemented**: there is no data-side hook for it. The only engine cost that exists is `SELL_CORE_STABILITY_COST` (-20 stability, seller only, core locations only).
- **The GUI takes a flat ceiling and nothing else.** `sell_location_action_view.gui` is a same-name full-file replacement (in `replaced_files.txt`) whose price `economy_slider` sets `max = 100` — a **literal**, replacing vanilla's `[SellLocationActionView.GetMaxPrice]` (the buyer's whole treasury). The engine still applies `GetMaxPrice` as a further clamp on top, so the effective ceiling is `min(100, buyer's treasury)`. The paired loc override `ars_belli_sell_location_l_english.yml` relabels `sell_buy_location_price`.
- **A *scaling* ceiling is impossible here — proven, do not retry.** The `max` property accepts only a literal or a bare getter on the view's own datacontext, no computed expression. Tested in game with four otherwise-identical sliders differing only in `max`: a literal (`9999`) works; `Multiply_float(SellLocationActionView.GetMaxPrice, 0.5)` — arithmetic over vanilla's own known-good source — does **not**; neither does `Player.GetTotalTaxBase` raw nor `FixedPointToFloat(Player.GetTotalTaxBase)`. `SellLocationActionView` exposes no tax-base getter (only `CanSend`, `GetAcceptance*`, `GetConfirmMessage`, `GetLocationsSortSearch`, `GetMax/MinPrice`, `GetPossibleLocations`, `GetTotalPrice`, `IsBuying`, `IsSelected`, `OnSend`, `SetTotalPrice`, `ToggleLocation`), and clamping the committed value is not available either (`onvaluechanged = SetTotalPrice`, no scripted intermediary). Hence the flat number.
- **Two silent-failure traps found here, both generalize.** (1) A `ranged_slider` whose `max` assignment is rejected keeps its default of **100** and logs nothing — a clean `error.log` does *not* mean a GUI property took effect; verify against a known value. (2) `FixedPointToFloat` on something already a float (e.g. `GetMaxPrice`) *does* log `FetchData failed` and kills the whole enclosing expression. Bisect GUI expressions with parallel widgets and a literal control rather than reasoning about which sub-term is wrong.
- `GetSeller` / `GetBuyer` appear in `diplomacy_l_english.yml` but in **no** vanilla `.gui`: localization and the GUI use separate data registries, so loc-only functions are unavailable to GUI expressions.
- **Gift scale for reference** (`prices\00_hardcoded.txt` → `send_gift`): `scaled_recipient_gold = 2`, `scaled_gold = 1`, `min_scale = 25` — i.e. 2 months of recipient income + 1 of sender. Not reachable from GUI script, which is why a flat number was used instead.

### 3i. Mercenary listing removed
- **The real feature is engine-side and the script action is a decoy.** What players use is the **"Set Up The Mercenary Contract"** window (`SETUP_MERCENARY`, the `setup_condottieri` lateralview) — engine-driven end to end: `SetupCondottieriView` / `CondottieriItem`, with `OnAccept`, `OnCostModifierChanged`, `OnDurationChanged`, `DelistUnhiredMercenaries` all engine functions, and refusals coming from engine loc keys (`MERC_UNIT_HAS_MERCENARIES` and siblings in `units_l_english.yml`, i.e. `NUnitUtilities::CanBecomeMercenary`), not from any script trigger. **No data change reaches it.** There is also a `make_unit_available_for_hire` generic_action with the same name and the same tooltip title — gating it does nothing to the player path. Two attempts were burned on that decoy.
- **There are FOUR player-facing surfaces, all GUI, all needing their own edit.** Closing one at a time cost three round trips with the player. All four are same-name replacements in `replaced_files.txt`:
  1. `army_builder.gui` — the "Become Mercenary" header button, the only caller of `ShowCondottieriViewWithFilter` (no hotkey reaches it). Killed by restoring `blockoverride "right_widget_visible" { visible = no }` — vanilla's own default from `shared\windows.gui`, which army_builder *empties* to switch the button on — and emptying `blockoverride "right_widget"{}` so the widget is never built.
  2. `single_unit_window.gui` — the individual army screen's action bar (`SingleUnitWindow.GetAllActionItems`). This is the one players actually use.
  3. `multi_unit_window.gui` — the same list for a multi-unit selection, plus the pinned quick-action row.
  4. `context_menu.gui` — the right-click unit menu (`QuickUnitActions.AccessList`).
- **Filtering an engine `UnitActionItem`:** `visible = "[Not(EqualTo_string(UnitActionItem.GetName, Localize('BECOME_MERCENARY')))]"`, ANDed onto whatever `visible` vanilla already had. `UnitActionItem.GetKey` **does** exist (vanilla uses it in `multi_unit_window.gui` for the pin collection) and would be sturdier, but the key's *value* is not discoverable from the files and a wrong guess fails silently — both halves of the name comparison are verifiable instead. In `single_unit_window.gui` the filter must go on each `unit_action_category`'s `unit_action_category_block_visible` blockoverride (7 of them), **not** on the item template: a blockoverride replaces the block wholesale, so a template default is overwritten by every category.
- `make_unit_available_for_hire` (generic_action) is *also* disabled by a **same-name full-file replacement** of `in_game\common\generic_actions\make_unit_available_for_hire.txt` (in `replaced_files.txt`) — it closes the AI/automation path (`generic_action_ai_lists\global_list.txt` lists it) but was never the player path. Renting out own regiments put the mercenary modifiers on the hirer while the owner kept the full hire price — a gold transfer that paid a profit.
- **Method note.** The thing that finally located it was grepping the *player-visible strings* (`"Set Up The Mercenary Contract"` → `SETUP_MERCENARY`, the refusal text → `MERC_UNIT_HAS_MERCENARIES`) rather than the action key. Ask for the exact on-screen wording early when a removal "doesn't work" — a matching action name is not proof you have the right mechanism.
- **`REPLACE:` in `generic_actions`: KEYWORD IS REAL, HONOURING IS UNPROVEN (revised 2026-11-11).** The engine binary (`binaries/eu5.exe`) contains the keyword table `INJECT:` / `TRY_INJECT:` / `INJECT_OR_CREATE:` / `REPLACE:` / `REPLACE_OR_CREATE:` as contiguous strings, so `REPLACE:` is genuine engine syntax — it is simply never used by vanilla or any DLC (0 occurrences, vs 12 `INJECT:` and 13 `INJECT_OR_CREATE:`). The original conclusion below rests on ONE unlogged failure, not on the keyword being fake; a per-database gap is still the best explanation, but it has not been re-tested. Since 2026-11-12 the religion override is a same-name full copy again (`general_religion.txt`, game 1.4.8, in `replaced_files.txt`), because a silent no-op was judged worse than a file to re-sync; the culture override stays a minimal `REPLACE:` file (`abm_culture.txt`) with a VERIFY-IN-GAME note in its header, as the test case for this database.
- **Original note — `REPLACE:` does not work in `generic_actions` — proven by a shipped failure, do not retry it here.** The first attempt (`17a2076`, `ars_belli_no_mercenary_listing.txt` holding `REPLACE:make_unit_available_for_hire` with `potential = { always = no }`) had **no effect whatsoever** and **logged nothing**; players could still open the action and list units. `REPLACE:` *is* honoured in other databases — `REPLACE:order_commandery` in `building_types` is live and the engine names it under the plain key in `error.log` (`Building 'order_commandery' has want_foreign_pop_created = yes…`, a property only the mod's copy had — since fixed by adding the 1.4 field, see the 1.4 error-log triage bullet). So treat `REPLACE:` as **per-database**, not universal: after using it, confirm the entry actually took rather than assuming.
- **Single-action edits in this folder (2026-11-11, revised 2026-11-12).** `general_religion.txt` (`convert_religion`: ruler's faith dropped, and a faith outside the country's religious group needs >75% of pops) is a same-name full copy of the game 1.4.8 file; only the lines marked `Ars Belli` differ. `abm_culture.txt` (`change_primary_culture`: ruler's culture dropped, target culture needs >=50% of pops and >75% when outside the country's own culture group) and `abm_religious_factions.txt` (`demand_extra_payment_from_shogun_court`, gold 24x -> 4x and opinion gate `>= -50` -> `> 0`) are small `REPLACE:` files with a VERIFY IN GAME header: if the edited behaviour does not appear, `REPLACE:` is not honoured in this database and each must become a same-name full copy too. Whole-file copies remain correct (and proven) for the many-actioned files this folder deletes outright - `middle_kingdom.txt`, `red_turban_rebellions.txt`, `rise_of_timur.txt`, `ilkhanate_actions.txt`, `crisis_of_the_chinese_dynasty_actions.txt`, `decline_of_majapahit_actions.txt` - where a per-key override cannot express the removal.
- **Gate a removed action at every stage, not one.** The shipped failure had `potential`, `ai_prerequisite` and the unit `select_trigger.visible` all set to `no` and still worked, which is what proved the whole entry was inert. The replacement sets `show_in_gui_list = no` (the readme flag that keeps an action out of the auto-generated GUI lists — **no `.gui` file references this action by name**, so the button is auto-generated), `potential`, `allow`, `ai_prerequisite`, `automation_tick = never`, the select_trigger, **and empties the `effect`** so nothing can be listed even if a path is missed.
- **`ai_tick = never` means "already handled in code"** per `country_interactions\readme.txt` — so if AI countries list mercenaries at all, it is engine code and no data change reaches it. Keep this reading of `ai_tick = never` in mind generally: it does not simply mean "AI never does this".
- `delist_unit` is a separate key in the same vanilla file and is kept byte-identical to vanilla, so already-listed units can be pulled back.

### 3p. Age-based mercenaries without retiring the professionals (2026-10-31)
- Part of the main mod (the separate `.tools\Mercenary Mod\` staging folder is gone; every file was moved in on 2026-10-31). Files: `in_game\common\unit_types\abm_tech_line_no_mercenaries.txt` (34 `REPLACE:<unit>`), `in_game\common\unit_types\abm_mercenary_unit_types.txt` (34 `a_abm_merc_<unit>`), `main_menu\localization\english\abm_merc_unit_types_l_english.yml` (names/descs alias `$a_<unit>$` / `$a_<unit>_desc$`, one leading space per entry). Naming prefix is **`abm`**, never `mm`.
- The four touched GUI files are full vanilla copies and live at their real vanilla paths: `in_game\gui\multi_unit_window.gui` (pre-existing "Become Mercenary" removal), `regiments_overview.gui`, `setup_mercenary_requirements.gui`, `in_game\gui\shared\combat_tooltips.gui` (the last three carry the age badge and are listed in `replaced_files.txt`). `main_menu\gui\` holds frontend GUI only; the mercenary files briefly sat there and were ignored by the game.
- Vanilla mercenaries use the location owner's researched unit. Here each of the 34 land units in `2_unlocked_through_tech.txt` that carry `mercenaries_per_location` gets a dedicated `a_abm_merc_<unit>`, gated to its age by `location_potential = { current_age = age_N }`. Levies, light dragoons and cuirassiers stay vanilla.
- **`copy_from` on an age template writes the changes back onto that template** (limits/age/hide then hit every professional and unique sharing it), so both files write category, combat stats, terrain (`impact`/`combat`, incl. the `river` line on heavy cavalry), artillery `bombard_efficiency`/`artillery_barrage`, maintenance/construction/gfx/upgrades in full. Multi-age (`age = { a b }`) is invalid and drops the file.
- `hide` is the retirement flag: templates set `hide = yes`, so a unit hides as soon as any later unit of its category exists (including a current-age mercenary). Professionals therefore carry **no** `hide`; only the `a_abm_merc_*` types do. Never add `max_strength`/`combat_power` on top of a template - they add.
- Retirement gate, written as the **first** line of `country_potential` on every professional that has `upgrades_to` (block created where vanilla had none): `NOT = { has_advance = <successor's unlock> }`. Keys come from `2_army_unlocks.txt` (`unlock_<unit>_advance`); the supply line has no `_advance` suffix (`unlock_supply_carts`, `unlock_supply_convoy`, `unlock_baggage_train`, `unlock_wagon_train`, `unlock_logistics_corps`). Age-6 units (sharpshooters, fusiliers, flying battery, logistics corps) have no successor and get no NOT line. `a_handgonners` uses `unlock_handgonners_advance` (`1_building_unlocks.txt`) by explicit instruction - note that advancing it also *unlocks* a_handgonners, so this line makes it unbuildable for any country holding that advance.
- Merc types: `is_special = yes`, `buildable = yes` (`buildable = no` keeps it out of the pool), `limit = 0`, `levy = no`, `hide = yes`, **no `age` line** (an age makes the merc the current unit of its category and retires the professional), the professional's government `country_potential` (evaluated on the location owner) minus the NOT line, and the vanilla `mercenaries_per_location` weights.
- The age badge in the UI is carried by a 0.001 naval stat, one per age: `anti_piracy_warfare` (1), `maritime_presence` (2), `blockade_capacity` (3), `hull_size` (4), `transport_capacity` (5), `crew_size` (6). `regiments_overview.gui`, `setup_mercenary_requirements.gui` and `shared\combat_tooltips.gui` test `HasStat(...)` on it to pick the `abm_age_badge_<n>` tooltip (and hide the vanilla `[GetAgeIcon(SubUnitType.GetAge)]` icon). Every branch is ANDed with `GetCategory.IsArmy`, so naval units never hit it and vanilla army units never carry those stats - without the `a_abm_merc_*` types the badge is inert.
- Every REPLACE block opens with `# Ars Belli: vanilla copy_from = <template>` and every retirement line carries a mark, so a re-sync can rebuild the unit from vanilla. Both files are in `replaced_files.txt`.
- Rebuilding the units also dropped the template's `buildable = no`. Tech still appears to gate them (Stabshot's age 3 screenshot shows only age 1 units on a country without later advances). Not checked on the merged mod.
- `a_footmen_levy` is the **only** type that `copy_from`s a professional, so it inherits the new `a_footmen` (loses the template's `hide`, gains the NOT line). Left deliberate - no REPLACE added.
- Unit types are read at startup: a full game restart is needed to see any of this.

### 3k. town_setups: all ABM changes moved into one REPLACE: file (2026-10-15)
- The mod used to ship a **full copy** of `in_game/common/town_setups/00_default.txt` (1791 lines vs vanilla 1.4's 1686; listed in `replaced_files.txt`). Comparison against vanilla 1.4: **0 vanilla templates missing**, 6 mod-only templates (`ryukyu_capital`, `ryukyu_town`, `steppe_rural_settlement`, `erenkhot_mongol_capital`, `prince_elector_capital_city`, `archbishop_elector_capital_city` - all referenced by `setup/1337/07_cities_and_buildings.txt`), **75 templates byte-identical** to vanilla, **45 rebalanced** (43 value raises - `tannery` x15, `paper_guild` x8, ... - 3 lowers, 27 added buildings, and 12 `castle` lines commented with `#NEW - removed due to fort adjecency rule`). No vanilla building was silently dropped.
- That file is gone; every change now lives in `in_game/common/town_setups/abm_town_setups.txt` as **45 `REPLACE:<name> = { <full body> }`** entries plus plain definitions for the 6 new templates and the 2 `abm_swahili_port_town*` templates from the earlier B6e fix (53 total). `replaced_files.txt` updated. Benefit: the 75 untouched templates now track vanilla, so a future vanilla update no longer needs a manual re-sync of this file.
- **Watch this on the next run**: `REPLACE:` is per-database and **fails silently** (proven in `generic_actions` - no effect, no log), and there is **no vanilla readme documenting it** (vanilla uses `REPLACE:` nowhere in `in_game/common/`; a workshop mod uses it for `diplomatic_costs`, the ABM uses it in 17 other folders including `building_types`, where it is proven live). So for `town_setups` it must be confirmed, not assumed. Free probe: the B6e rows `tannery in Suceava ... Current level is 2 (Max is 1)` and the same for Kursk exist **only because** the mod's `carpathian_town`/`russian_town` set `tannery = 2` (vanilla = 1). Rows still present => `REPLACE:` took; rows gone => it was ignored, and the fix is `git checkout HEAD -- in_game/common/town_setups/00_default.txt`.
- **Merge `feature/steppes-update` (2026-10-18):** that branch still edited the deleted full copy (buffed `kaffa_city` + added `large_steppe_settlement`/`cossacks_village`). Resolved by keeping `00_default.txt` deleted and porting the three blocks into `abm_town_setups.txt` (`REPLACE:kaffa_city` updated in place, the two new templates added next to `steppe_rural_settlement`). Their branch had `large_steppe_settlement` defined **twice** and `tomakivka`/`rozdory` duplicated in `07_cities_and_buildings.txt` — deduped. Rule for future merges: any edit to `00_default.txt` on another branch must be ported this way, never by restoring the file.
- **The `07_cities_and_buildings.txt` `enice` trap:** `enice` is listed twice in vanilla (a `town` in the Crimea block and a `rural_settlement` in the "#NEW - Horde eco buffs" block). The Crimea entry must stay **commented out** because ABM's `REPLACE:kurultai` is `rural_settlement = yes / town = no / city = no`, so a town `enice` makes its starting kurultai illegal.
### 3j. Selling works of art — AI willingness
- **Four gold-for-art paths exist in the whole game**, and no others (`grep destroy_art|move_art_and_owner|art_price` over `in_game\`): `country_interactions\sell_work_of_art.txt` (offer to a country, they accept/decline), `country_interactions\request_work_of_art_purchase.txt` (the mirror, you buy), `generic_actions\sell_work_of_art_to_estates.txt` (instant, no counterparty, 60% of `art_price`), and the Orthodox-only `country_interactions\sell_icon.txt`.
- **`art_price` is engine-computed and not moddable.** No define, no entry in `prices\`; `price:sell_work_of_art = { gold = 1 }` is only a stub that `price_modifier` overwrites with `scope:target.art_price`. Its magnitude relative to a country's income is **not knowable from script** — assume nothing about it.
- **Vanilla's acceptance formula never looked at the price.** Opinion, the buyer's own treasury and the art's quality only. So a rich AI bought nearly anything at whatever the engine valued it at, which is the free-money faucet. Ars Belli's `sell_work_of_art.txt` (a **same-name full-file replacement**, in `replaced_files.txt` — it was a `REPLACE:` until 3i proved that unreliable per-database) adds a price-against-income term, drops `diplo_chance.base` −30 → −50, halves `WEALTHY_BUYER` and raises its bar 24 → 36 months, adds a −25 band for `art_quality` 40–69 (vanilla punished only below 40) and deepens the below-40 band −50 → −90.
- **`diplo_chance.base` and the `accept` block sum into one acceptance score** — see also 3f. `accept` factor `desc =` values are plain loc keys.
- **`divide = { value = X min = 1 }` is the vanilla divide-by-zero guard** (`script_values\diplomatic_values.txt`), and `max = N` as a sibling of `value`/`multiply` clamps the finished term. Both are worth reaching for whenever an acceptance term divides by another country's income.
- **Estates and "the bank" are the same thing in EU5's own vocabulary** — `generic_actions\take_bank_loan.txt` defines `take_estate_loan`, whose message log reads "We took a loan from the bank". Expect player reports about "banks" to mean either the estates or a counterparty country; ask which.

### 3k. Intervene in War & Threaten War removed; Enforce Peace is the replacement
- **Both are engine actions.** No `country_interactions` file, no `prices` entry, no `on_action`. `intervene_in_war` is only ever named in GUI as `[OpenDiploAction('intervene_in_war')]`; `threaten_war` only as an `ai_diplochance` weight. The moddable surface is: the `allowed_*` country modifiers, the loc, and the GUI.
- **Threaten War is a clean kill** — `allowed_threaten_war` is the engine's only gate (vanilla `THREATEN_WAR_NOT_POWERFUL_ENOUGH` says exactly that: "We do not have the [allowed_threaten_war] modifier"). Set `no` on `REPLACE:is_great_power` **and** `REPLACE:is_regional_power` (`main_menu\common\static_modifiers\abm_country_changes.txt`) and on the HRE `leader_modifier` (`in_game\common\international_organizations\hre.txt`). Those are the only three grants in the whole game.
- **Intervene in War is NOT.** `allowed_intervene_in_war = no` closes only the rank path. The engine hands the action to **any** country whose rival is in the war, whatever the modifier says — vanilla admits it in `INTERVENE_IN_WAR_NOT_POWERFUL_ENOUGH`: *"We are not powerful enough to intervene in the wars that countries that are not our rivals."* There is no data-side flag for the rival path. **If a report says Intervene is still usable, this is why.**
- **The rival path is closed in the GUI instead** (same-name replacements in `replaced_files.txt`):
  1. `in_game\gui\select_war_to_intervene.gui` — the engine's war picker (`SelectInterveneWindow.GetWars`), rewritten with **no list at all**, just a message and Cancel. **This is the whole removal**: it is the choke point every remaining path runs through, so no war can be selected and the action can never complete, rival or not. 44 lines, and the reason no bigger GUI file has to be replaced.
  2. `in_game\gui\context_menu.gui` — tag guard `Not(EqualTo_string(DiplomaticActionItem.GetTag, 'INTERVENE_IN_WAR'))` on the pinned quick-action entry, plus `in_game\gui\war_lateralview.gui` where both Intervene buttons are `visible = no`.
- **`DiplomaticActionItem.GetTag` is the loc key prefix in caps**, i.e. `INTERVENE_IN_WARTITLE` → tag `INTERVENE_IN_WAR` (same for `UNCONDITIONALSURRENDER`, `IMPROVE_RELATION`, …). `EqualTo_string` against a wrong tag is harmless (just false), unlike `IsType`, whose accepted strings are a different, lowercase set (`declarewar`, `requestpeace`, `create_casus_belli`).
- **The row is left in the diplomacy list on purpose — do not "fix" it by replacing `diplomacy_macrobuilder_lateralview.gui`.** Gating it there works (`type ui_diplomatic_action_button`, the template behind both the quick actions and the categorised list `foreign_country_lateralview.gui` renders via `GetCategoryItems` → `diplomatic_actions`), but that is a 2400-line vanilla file to re-merge on every patch and it was **explicitly rejected as not worth the maintenance**. The row stays, reads "Intervene in War (Removed)" from the loc override, and opens the dead picker. Hiding it is not an option either: `diplomatic_actions` is a `fixedgridbox`, so an invisible item still eats its grid cell and leaves a hole in Hostile Actions.
- **Loc overrides live in `main_menu\localization\english\replace\01_abm_locals_vanilla_l_english.yml`** — the engine keeps listing both actions, so `INTERVENE_IN_WARTITLE` / `THREATEN_WARTITLE` read "(Removed)", the `_DESC` / `_FLAVOR` / `_TOOLTIP_HEADER*` / `_NOT_POWERFUL_ENOUGH` keys say what replaced them, and `CRANK_CAN_INTERVENE` / `CRANK_CAN_THREATEN_WAR` (the rank-tooltip bullets) no longer advertise them.
- **Engine Enforce Peace is off too** (`allowed_enforce_peace = no` on both ranks and the HRE leader) because it is a single attacker-side accept/decline. **It still survives on vanilla situation league leaders** — `foreign_league_balkan/france/hre/iberia` and `italian_league_1/2/3` each set `allowed_enforce_peace = yes` in their own `leader_modifier`, and those files are not replaced. That is why the `ENFORCE_PEACETITLE` override still carries the against-the-rules warning. Unions have a separate `union_allowed_enforce_peace` modifier + `union_enforce_peace` country_interaction, untouched.
- **The replacement is `ars_belli_enforce_peace`** (`in_game\common\country_interactions\`, events in `in_game\events\ars_belli_enforce_peace_events.txt`, loc in `in_game\localization\english\ars_belli_enforce_peace_l_english.yml`). Defender is asked first; only on their acceptance does the attacker get `ars_belli_enforce_peace.1`; refusal drags the enforcer into the war on the defender's side. Standing: `is_mp_gp`, `is_mp_major`, or rival to either war leader — deliberately the same reach Intervene had. The vanilla rival-war alert (`can_intervene_in_rival_war`) is retargeted to it in `alertmanager.gui` with the `ALERT_INTERVENE_RIVAL_WAR_*` keys rewritten.
- **Italian Wars `iw_intervene_in_war` is a different mechanic** (a situation generic_action for joining the Italian Wars, `generic_actions\italian_wars.txt`). Untouched — do not confuse the two when grepping for "intervene".

### 3l. Player Coalition (parallel to vanilla coalition)
- New non-unique IO type `abm_player_coalition` (`in_game\common\international_organizations\abm_player_coalition.txt`). Vanilla `coalition` untouched, both coexist.
- Players only: `create_visible/enabled` + `can_join_trigger` check `is_ai = no`; `ai_desire_to_join` = -1000. NO `is_ai` in auto_leave on purpose (MP disconnect would kick player).
- DON'T put `is_ai` in `join_visible_trigger` / `invite_visible_trigger`: root there is not reliably the joiner; it hid the panel's Ask-to-join action (greyed, totally empty tooltip = action not built). `ai_desire_to_allow_new_member` = +20 (not -1000) so an AI-run leader (disconnected / tag-switch test) still accepts players.
- Engine needs `io_opinion_<type>` bias (`biases\abm_player_coalition_opinion.txt`) + loc `diplomatic_status_<type>_name/_tooltip`, `<type>_list_who_tt`, else error.log at load.
- Relaxed join: `abm_can_join_player_coalition_against` (`scripted_triggers\abm_player_coalition_triggers.txt`) drops vanilla opinion<-50, knows_country, diplo range, limited diplomacy, not-at-war. Keeps: antagonism>50 (re-added by user request), not rebel, not subject (tributary ok), no subject link w/ target, no truce, no union, not already in one vs target, anti_coalition_treaty. auto_leave = vanilla incl. antagonism<30 exit (re-added by user request; only when not at war with target).
- Target rank: `can_target_trigger` = `is_mp_gp` OR `is_mp_major` (user: GP + Major since mod.25; was GP only). `potential_target_trigger` ALSO = the same OR — readme says alert-only but engine checks it on the create action: `always = no` showed as a red "Never" requirement and blocked creation (verified in game).
- Create/invite/join buttons are ENGINE-generated per IO type (generic `CREATE_IO_TARGETTING`, `INVITE_IO`, `ASKJOIN_IO`, `JOIN_IO_TARGETTING` loc; vanilla coalition has its own `*_COALITION*` variants). No country_interaction needed.
- CB = vanilla `cb_coalition` (user choice; a custom take-capital -40% CB was built then dropped). LEADER ONLY: given in `on_creation` to scope:actor (+ on_joined fallback if root = leader_country). Juniors get no CB, else they could declare from the normal Declare War screen and lead the war. on_left removes it only if not also in a vanilla coalition vs same target.
- Leader must be war leader: NO `promote_strongest_member_to_war_leader` (vanilla coalition has it; it made a stronger junior the war leader). Panel Rally button is leader-only via `IsIOLeaderCountry(GetPlayer)` (pattern from vanilla independence_movement.gui). Reverse edge NOT handled: leaving a vanilla coalition (vanilla on_left) strips cb_coalition even if still in a player coalition.
- Peace treaty `abm_dissolve_player_coalition` mirrors vanilla `dissolve_coalition` (anti_coalition_treaty 15y), gated on loser in abm_player_coalition + war CB cb_coalition. Vanilla one only checks type=coalition.
- Panel `in_game\gui\panels\organization\abm_player_coalition.gui` = vanilla coalition.gui copy w/ 2 loc keys swapped; icon `main_menu\gfx\interface\icons\international_organizations\abm_player_coalition.dds` = coalition.dds copy. Re-copy panel on vanilla gui updates.
- `alliance_can_exist` (abm_alliance_overrides) + `abm_vision_sharing` also block vs player-coalition target, same as vanilla coalition.
- Loc: `main_menu\localization\english\abm_player_coalition_l_english.yml`.

### 3n. Focus Picks (age focus overhaul)
- Engine adm/dip/mil focus (`set_age_preference` + advance `for = X`) = ONE slot, 3 values. Can't do 2 picks. So dropped fully; system is script-side.
- Per age 2-6: Economic pick (3 branches) then Military pick (3 branches). NO naval-only branch (redesign 2026-10-24, community ask): naval stat go INSIDE land branch of same idea, same advance as army twin when can (Quantity: manpower+sailors, Quality: tactics+naval dmg taken...). Mil branches: II Levy/Mercenary/Standing Forces, III Quality/Quantity/Leadership, IV Offense/Defense/Balanced, V Maneuver/Logistics/Firepower, VI Voluntary Service/Conscription/Insurrection. Max 6 advance per branch (user want less research). No good 3rd idea -> "Balanced" mix (user rule).
- MP meta (user): armies cav-only till Absolutism (age V), then infantry-only. Morale matter for infantry (lose by retreat), little for cav (fight to death). Galley good only in inland sea. So no combined-arms stuff (MP never mix). Value cav power early, infantry power/morale late. Design source: `Ars Belli MP Mod - Design Doc - Focus Picks.md` (user-local, gitignored).
- PANEL LOOK (2026-10-25, generated by guiFile()): vanilla window (bg_lateralview + window_header_alt color_advances + button_close_alt, Esc close), intro paper card w/ Clausewitz quote, one tab per age (button_secondary_tab_alt; client var `abm_focus_age` = 2..6, unset -> current age via SGUI `abm_focus_age_default_<age>`), age banner = vanilla age illustration (illustrations/advances/<age>.dds) + age icon + status (SGUI `abm_focus_age_current/reached_<age>`), Eco/Mil strip (color_economy/military tint, "Picked: X"), cards = branch `art` header (focus_data `art:` = vanilla illustration path) + advance icon rows (loc `abm_focus_adv.<key>` = ShowAdvanceName link). Only vanilla textures; generator gfx() THROWS if a texture path missing. User saw it in-game 2026-10-25, works. Pick card art by LOOKING: `node .tools/scripts/dds_sheet.js out.png 244 92 6 <dds...>` -> PNG sheet (DXT1/5 only, BC7 skipped). AVOID illustrations/bg/* (faint window backgrounds: dark/greyscale on cards).
- Flow (panel picking): hardcoded `on_new_age` fires `ages_of_eu.1` (mod `in_game/events/ages.txt`, same-name replace) = HIDDEN dispatcher, skipped in age 1. AI NEVER picks (user decision: a disconnected player must not get AI picks; SGUIs have no ai_is_valid so AI can't use them either). Player (is_ai = no): notice event `abm_focus_picks.<age n>` (2-6, one per age so each has its own `image =`, the age's namesake institution art; vanilla `illustration_tags = { ages }` only has art in the age window -> empty frame in a country_event) -> pick in Focus Picks panel. Panel cards: Pick button (SGUI `abm_focus_pick_<b>`: shown = slot empty, valid = age reached via OR of current_age >= branch age) and Change button (SGUI `abm_focus_switch_<b>`: shown = sibling holds slot, valid = `abm_focus_changes_used` < 3; removes siblings, sets var, +1 used). `abm_focus_pending` SGUI drives always-visible top-right button ("Focus Picks (!)"). Changes left shown via custom loc `abm_focus_changes_left`. SGUI calls in GUI: `GetScriptedGui(k).IsShown/IsValid/Execute(GuiScope.SetRoot(GetPlayer.MakeScope).End)` (IsShown/IsValid untested before this; Execute proven by tier panel).
- Focus change rule: EU5 has NO un-research effect (only `research_advance`); advance hidden by potential KEEPS its bonuses (tested: Enforced Service levy size stayed after switching Levy->Mercenary). So `abm_focus_switch_<b>` is valid only while none of the siblings' advances is researched (user chose this over counter-modifiers). Top-bar button lives in the rank block next to Tier List (a copy anchored below the bar was clipped/invisible) + standalone copy when MP rule off. Greyed buttons = separate enabled=no twin with reason tooltip.
- Pick events are `country_event`, NOT `age_event`. Age window (eventwindow.gui AgeEventWindow) = 3 fixed cards via `AccessOptionItemWithKey('adm'/'dip'/'mil')` + hardcoded Adm/Dip/Mil tooltips; custom options have no key -> every card shows option #1. Don't switch back.
- Pick = `set_variable = abm_focus_<branch>` (never cleared -> old-age picks stay researchable). Advance gate = `potential = { has_variable = abm_focus_<branch> }`. potential IS re-checked in game (user-verified) despite vanilla readme "not retroactively visible" -> unpicked branches hidden, like vanilla `for = X`. Earlier `allow` version kept all 36/age visible -> clutter + orphans auto-attached under them.
- Vanilla `4_choices_adm/dip/mil.txt` emptied (same-name). Kept advances redefined under VANILLA KEYS in `abm_focus_picks_economic.txt` / `_military.txt` — keys referenced by vanilla script: exploration (CB), privateers (naval laws, OMA event), military_drill, innovativeness, humanism, battlefield_commisions, smithian_economics, national_bank. Don't rename those. "AND" combos keep first key; second key dropped.
- Tree shape per branch: adv0 root (`depth = 0`, works on NEW game; advance tree changes do not apply to old saves, test on fresh start); 1->2(->6) off 0; 3->4->5 off 0 (`PARENT` in generator; branches may have 5-7 advances, e.g. Levy/Quality/Naval 7, Voluntary Service 5). ~237 vanilla/mod advances in ages 2-6 have no requires/depth (units, buildings, country advances) -> engine auto-places them under any VISIBLE node; `allow_children = no` does NOT stop it (tested). Hiding unpicked branches via potential is the fix. Vanilla age roots mostly institution-gated, don't hang branches under them.
- Event option names dotted (`abm_focus_picks.<branch>`) like vanilla. Raw option key atop option tooltip = debug-mode only (vanilla shows it too), not a bug.
- `infiltrate_administration_advance` (vanilla diplomacy_unlocks.txt) moved to Espionage via REPLACE:. `mercenaries_reformation_advance` NOT moved (country advances CAS/TUR/India require it) -> new `abm_ensure_mercenary_payments` instead.
- **Lesser Infiltration** (general Renaissance tree, advance `abm_lesser_infiltration_advance` after `spy_construction_renaissance`) = scripted stand-in for the engine action: one-way relation `abm_lesser_infiltration` with `lifts_fog_of_war = yes` (readme: relations can lift FoW; engine Infiltrate Administration itself = fixed 50 spy network in diplomatic_costs/00_hardcoded.txt, no cost modifier, not scriptable). Cost `abm_lesser_infiltration_cost` = 50 spy network (same as engine); 3 months = 1/8 of engine 24 via timed variable list `abm_lesser_infiltration_targets` (days = 91) + will_expire_trigger; break_on_not_spying; AI off. UNTESTED: which side of a oneway relation gets the FoW lift.
- `steal_technology` same-name copy, allow `has_advance = abm_industrial_espionage`. Target no longer loses research progress (vanilla -10% removed); actor still gains 20% of the target's progress.
- **1.4 port (2026-10-28)**: audited all values vs vanilla 1.3.11 + 1.4.4. Vanilla rescales fixed: `land_morale_movement_cost` x7 (Trained March, Unrelenting Advance -0.0002 -> -0.0014), devastation recovery halved (People's War 0.01 -> 0.005), National Trade Policy -> `small_selling_efficiency_bonus`, Privateers + `unlock_policy = issue_letter_of_marque_policy`. Smithian follows vanilla constant cut (5% -> 2.5%). 1.4 moved set_policy / hire_advisor / accepted+primary culture prices to Political Influence, so Empiricism -66% set policy etc. now = PI discounts. No vanilla `for = X` advances outside 4_choices; no vanilla refs to dropped 4_choices keys.
- **GENERATED FILES**: advances x2, `in_game/events/ages.txt`, `main_menu/localization/english/abm_focus_picks_l_english.yml` all from `.tools/scripts/focus_picks/focus_data.js` via `node .tools/scripts/focus_picks/generate_focus_picks.js`. Edit data, re-run. Never hand-edit outputs.
- **PUBLIC WEB PAGE** of all picks: https://claude.ai/artifact/29vc2LhdPrB3yGwZ2P5MQT, built by `node .tools/scripts/focus_picks/page/build.js` (reads focus_data.js + vanilla modifier names/formats). Any focus change -> add entry on top of `page/changelog.json` (ISO time of change, title, items with in-game modifier names, old -> new), rebuild, republish to SAME url. Page shows times in CET/CEST (Europe/Berlin). Output `page/focus-picks.html` is generated, don't hand-edit, but KEEP IT IN GIT (user want) -> commit rebuilt html with focus change. 2ND PAGE (user design aid, private): military modifiers reference https://claude.ai/artifact/UPKhXnW7GfJZbknTMFNH3Q, `node .tools/scripts/focus_picks/page/build_military.js` -> military-modifiers.html (tracked). Show every army/navy modifier + vanilla advance values + which focus picks use it -> rebuild + republish it too after focus change.
- Value rule used: reused vanilla advance keeps vanilla number unless doc says "(was X)". `*_efficiency` modifiers show ~half in UI (0.2 = "-10% cost"), so new efficiency values = 2x doc %.
- No engine modifier for: unit starting experience (Thorough Training -> monthly_experience_gain), artillery move speed (Limbers -> army_movement_speed 0.05), flat monthly manpower (Guerillas -> max_manpower 1). Stand-ins, verify in game.
- Modifier/icon validation: modifier keys = `MODIFIER_TYPE_NAME_*` in vanilla `modifier_types_l_english.yml` (engine modifiers have no def file). Icon must be an existing advance icon name.

### 3o. Battle simulator and army meta (2026-10-30)
- Simulator `.tools/scripts/battle_sim/` (`sim.js`, `engine.js`). Rules + tables: `EU5 Combat Mechanics (1.4.4).md`, 11b = 1.4, 11c = 1.3.11.
